- **zephyr-power-performance**: Power management and performance optimization for Zephyr RTOS. - **zephyr-pytest**: Write tests that run off the device in Python and drive it from outside, using twister_harness, usually end to end over a shell backdoor. If the test only needs to check C logic on the device, use zephyr-ztest instead. - **zero-inflated-count-simulation**: Use when you need synthetic zero-inflated, over-dispersed scRNA-seq counts with known true parameters (ln(mu), pi) to benchmark a model's robustness to misspecification of K, V, or dispersion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zero-inflated-count-simulation))
- **zero-inflated-dimensionality-reduction**: Use when scRNA-seq count data show dropout-driven zero inflation and PCA components appear driven by technical effects (sequencing depth, detection rate), and you need a low-dimensional representation reflecting biology. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zero-inflated-dimensionality-reduction))
- **zero-inflated-gamma-mixture-fitting**: Use when building scPower expression priors from a pilot UMI count matrix: you have pseudobulk-normalized gene means per cell type and need a zero-inflated gamma model over the means to later compute expression probabilities at chosen read depths. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/zero-inflated-gamma-mixture-fitting))
- **zero-inflated-gaussian-model-fitting**: Use when metagenomic/metatranscriptomic gene-cluster abundance or expression tables (e.g. RPKM counts from BiG-MAP mapping) have many zero-inflated values and you need parametric differential testing between grouped samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/zero-inflated-gaussian-model-fitting))
- **zero-inflated-mixture-likelihood-fitting**: Use when modeling continuous scRNA-seq expression of a target gene as a mixture over candidate TF logic combinations, where scRNA-seq dropouts require a point mass at zero and per-rule likelihoods (as in Loregic/LogicTRN) would be computationally prohibitive. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zero-inflated-mixture-likelihood-fitting))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-count-simulation**: Use when benchmarking DE or gene-set enrichment methods on scRNA-seq data and you need null count matrices with realistic zero inflation, or when calibration of null p-values across coverage rates must be tested. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zero-inflated-negative-binomial-count-simulation))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-data-generation**: Use when building simulated scRNA-seq benchmarks that require excess zeros plus overdispersed counts with within-sample correlation, mirroring the TWO-SIGMA simulation design (10 individuals x 1000 cells, ZINB with random effects). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/zero-inflated-negative-binomial-data-generation))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-gene-sampling**: Use when benchmarking perturbation-analysis methods like ICAT and you need synthetic control/perturbed AnnData pairs with known ground-truth perturbed cell states and tunable perturbation severity. - **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-mixed-model-fitting**: Use when raw non-negative integer scRNA-seq counts (gene x cell matrix) must be modeled with excess zeros and overdispersion, while controlling cell-level/subject-level covariates and accommodating within-sample correlation across cells from the same individual. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/zero-inflated-negative-binomial-mixed-model-fitting))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-mixed-modeling**: Use when analyzing raw (unnormalized) scRNA-seq count matrices with excess zeros and overdispersion, especially when cells are clustered within samples and covariate association testing (e.g., disease status) must account for within-sample correlation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/zero-inflated-negative-binomial-mixed-modeling))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-mixture-fitting**: Use when you have preprocessed binned bivariate read-count tables (e.g. histoneHMM BN.txt/SHR.txt) and need to reconstruct or fit the emission densities of a bivariate HMM for differential histone modification analysis. - **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-regression**: Use when raw integer scRNA-seq count data exhibit excess zeros and overdispersion, cells are nested within samples (within-sample correlation), and inference is needed on covariates such as a binary disease status beyond a two-group comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/zero-inflated-negative-binomial-regression))
- **zero-inflated-negative-binomial-regression**: Use when comparing single-cell Hi-C normalized read counts between two conditions (rows = bin pairs, columns = cells) and you need statistically principled DCI calls that handle the excessive zeros typical of scHi-C data.
